a1-cos-4-at-2 · by referee (from the manuscript)REFUTED

cos(4) = 1 mod 16 in Z_2 (line 1 of the manuscript's table).

Scenario A: a referee checks a table of 2-adic and 3-adic values (docs/ux/brief-3-claim-ledger.md)

Script (driver lines)
  1  prec 4
  2  cos_at 4 with 2
Expected and actual, line by line · exit status expected 0 · written 0
#script lineexpectedlibrary
2cos_at 4 with 22: 1 + O(2^4)2: 9 + O(2^4) ≠
base-2 digits, lowest 4: expected ...0001, library ...1001; the digit of 2^3 is the first that differs
Footprint
R2357...

places of the claim: 2; nothing said about any other place.

Argument (argued, not computed)

The manuscript's value. At a prime the setting prec is the absolute p-adic precision N (tools/adf/README.md:249-251), so "cos(4) = 1 mod 16" is the line 2: 1 + O(2^4) at prec 4. The series of cos converges on 4 Z_2 (tools/adf/README.md:468-469). docs/SPEC.md:587 records the checked vector cos 4 = 9 mod 16, so the manuscript's line is expected to fail.

a2-v2-log-3 · by referee (from the manuscript)SEALED

v_2(log 3) = 2 in Q_2.

Scenario A: a referee checks a table of 2-adic and 3-adic values (docs/ux/brief-3-claim-ledger.md)

Script (driver lines)

Expected and actual, line by line · exit status expected 0 · written 0
#script lineexpectedlibrary
2log_at 3 with 22: 4 + O(2^3)2: 4 + O(2^3) =
Footprint
R2357...

places of the claim: 2; nothing said about any other place.

Argument (argued, not computed)

v_2(x) = 2 exactly when x = 4 u with u odd, that is when x = 4 mod 8 (4 u = 4 mod 8 for odd u, and x = 4 mod 8 gives x = 4 (1 + 2 k)). So the claim is the line 2: 4 + O(2^3) at prec 3. It is a checked test vector of docs/SPEC.md:587.

Argument (argued, not computed)

Two values equal modulo 3^8 print the same line at prec 8. The centre 958 of exp(3) modulo 3^8 was computed in exact rationals by ux/ledger/oracle.py (python3 ux/ledger/oracle.py 3 3 8), not read from the driver; the claim is that exp(12) prints the same line. Claim a5 explains the result, and claim a6 records FLINT's own equality test (docs/SPEC.md:605-608).

Argument (argued, not computed)

At a prime, abs X with p is |r|_p for the idele (x ; r * u) (tools/adf/README.md:367), so the real coordinate does not matter there and is 1. 6/35 = 2 * 3 / (5 * 7) gives 1/2, 1/3, 5, 7, and |6/35|_p = 1 for every prime p > 7, since v_p(6/35) = 0 (argued; 11 is computed as a sample). The real coordinate of the idele of 6/35 cannot be typed: a real part is a decimal ball (docs/conventions.md 9.1-9.2), so the last line uses the ball 0.171428571428571428571428571 +/- 1e-27, which contains 6/35. Its norm is |x_inf| / r (tools/adf/README.md:362), a ball that contains (6/35)/(6/35) = 1, the product of the absolute values over all places. The expected text 1 +/- 8.4e-27 was PROMOTED FROM THE SCRATCHPAD (the driver's own output): this line seals that the build reproduces it, and a reader checks that the ball contains 1; the driver has no set predicate for real balls that could decide "contains 1".

The computed primes check the rule; the cited sources argue it for every odd p.

  1  # Every status the library can return through the driver, one command per status.
  2  # Expected lines written from docs/conventions.md 3.1 and the headers.
  3  #
  4  # Eight of the eleven codes are reachable: ADF_OK, ADF_PARSE, ADF_LIMIT, ADF_NOT_UNIT,
  5  # ADF_NO_SOLUTION, ADF_NOT_UNIQUE, ADF_DOMAIN and ADF_UNSUPPORTED.  The other three are
  6  # not, and no command of this file can make them appear:
  7  #   ADF_NOT_DETERMINED       stage 7 of conventions 8.5 needs a sign condition of 9.3, and
  8  #                            the only such types are the idele, the idele class and the A
  9  #                            of a term; adele.h line 144 says of adf_adele_set_str that
 10  #                            "adf_adele has no sign condition, so ADF_NOT_DETERMINED does
 11  #                            not occur";
 12  #   ADF_UNIT_NOT_CERTIFIED   belongs to the inversion of an adele-like value, which SPEC
 13  #                            4.5 does not offer and the driver does not implement;
 14  #   ADF_NEEDS_SPLIT          belongs to the quotient by Q and to the functions at places
 15  #                            (SPEC 6, 9.3), which are work packages 1.9 and later.
 16  # ADF_LIMIT is reached three times: the line limit of the driver (the hostile case h4), the
 17  # decimal exponent over max_exp10, the binary exponent of a real ball over
 18  # ADF_PRINT_EXP_MAX (the guard of tools/adf/README.md, M1-D6), and a setting above the limit
 19  # the driver puts on it (prec over ADF_PRINT_EXP_MAX; a resource limit, conventions 3.1).
 20  #!exit 1

  2  # of a value with a real or complex part returns NULL with length 0 when the midpoint or the
  3  # radius of one of its real balls is not zero and has a binary exponent above
  4  # ADF_PRINT_EXP_MAX = 100000 in absolute value.  The binary exponent is the one FLINT stores,
  5  # x = m 2^e with 0.5 <= |m| < 1 (refs/src/flint-3.0.1/arf.rst:14-20), which is one more
  6  # than the exponent floor(log2 |x|) of the other reading; a mag holds the same form
  7  # (refs/src/flint-3.0.1/mag.rst:3-6).  So 2^99999 = 0.5 * 2^100000 has e = 100000, the last

  9  # bound of its own: it answers error: LIMIT for a printer that returns NULL.

fx-12_status_order · by tests/driver (imported by import_fixtures.py)SEALED

The order of the checks of one command (tools/adf/README.md, "The order of the checks").

The driver fixtures: tests/driver/*.cmd with their expected lines, as claims

Script (driver lines)
the whole script, 51 lines
  1  # The order of the checks of one command (tools/adf/README.md, "The order of the checks").
  2  # The driver decides in this order, and stops at the first step that fails:
  3  #   1. the line: an operation word of the table and the right number of operands, else PARSE;
  4  #   2. every operand, in order: the syntax of the value form alone, adf_text_classify
  5  #      (conventions 8.5, stages 1 to 3), else PARSE, or LIMIT for a text over max_len;
  6  #   3. every operand, in order: its kind; a kind of 9.7 with no typed parser in this build is
  7  #      UNSUPPORTED, before any value of the line is read;
  8  #   4. every operand, in order: its value, by the typed parser of its kind (conventions 8.5,
  9  #      stages 4 to 7), else LIMIT, DOMAIN or NOT_DETERMINED;
 10  #   5. the operation: the pair of types, the domain of the operation, else DOMAIN, NOT_UNIT,
 11  #      NO_SOLUTION or NOT_UNIQUE;
 12  #   6. the printer: a printer that returns NULL gives LIMIT (M1-D6).
 13  # A line that is not a sentence of the grammar of 9.2 is decided on the syntax of its operands
 14  # first: that is a fact about the line, and no other operand makes it a sentence.  A kind that
 15  # this build does not implement is decided before any value is read: a request on a type that
 16  # version 1 does not implement is not a domain error (conventions 3.1, ADF_UNSUPPORTED).
 17  #!exit 1
 18  # Step 3 before step 4: 1/0 is a sentence of the value form (a rat whose denominator is 0),
 19  # and rfun() is a sentence (the empty function of the kind rfun), whose kind has no typed parser.  The zero
 20  # denominator, which the typed parser would report as DOMAIN, is not read.
 21  add rfun() with 1/0
 22  # The same with the operands in the other order: the status does not depend on which operand
 23  # has the unimplemented kind.
 24  add 1/0 with rfun()
 25  # The same: max_exp10 = 100000 is a limit of stage 4, read at step 4, after the kind of every
 26  # operand has been decided.
 27  add rfun() with (1e100001 ; 0)
 28  # Step 3 for the second end point of the interval of reconstruct: it is a sentence of the
 29  # value form, of a kind with no typed parser.  (Before this repair the two end points went
 30  # straight to adf_rat_set_str and the line was PARSE.)
 31  reconstruct (* ; 1 mod 2) with rfun() with 1
 32  reconstruct (* ; 1 mod 2) with 0 with rfun()
 33  # Step 5: both kinds are implemented and both values are read; the pair of types is not one
 34  # that SPEC 4.1 combines, or the second operand is of the wrong kind for the operation.
 35  cap (* ; 3 mod 12) with (* ; 1 mod 2)
 36  reconstruct (* ; 1 mod 2) with (* ; 0 mod 1) with 1
 37  reconstruct (* ; 1 mod 2) with (0 ; 0) with 1
 38  # Step 2 before step 3: @@@ is not a sentence of the grammar of 9.2, so the line is PARSE
 39  # whichever other operand it holds.
 40  add (@@@ ; 0) with rfun()
 41  add rfun() with @@@
 42  # Step 4 before step 5: both kinds are implemented, and the value of the first operand is read
 43  # before the value of the second.  The exponent of 1e100001 is over max_exp10 (a limit of
 44  # stage 4), and the zero denominator of the other operand is a constraint of stage 6.
 45  add (1e100001 ; 0) with (1 ; 1/0)
 46  # The other way round: the first value read is the one that fails, so its status is reported
 47  # and the limit of the second operand is not reached.  The finite part of an adele of the
 48  # value form is q(a) or q(a) mod q(N), never a literal with an exponent, so the second
 49  # operand of the next line is a sentence of the grammar with a zero denominator.
 50  add (1 ; 1/0) with (1e100001 ; 0)
 51  add (1 ; 1) with (1 ; 1/0)

fx-char-class · by tests/driver (imported by import_fixtures.py)SEALED

SPEC 5, api-3c 3/7, CV-58, ideles P15. Expected values are derived here before running.

The driver fixtures: tests/driver/*.cmd with their expected lines, as claims

Script (driver lines)
  1  #!exit 1
  2  # SPEC 5, api-3c 3/7, CV-58, ideles P15. Expected values are derived here before running.
  3  # t=2, u'=-1; odd chi_3(2)(-1)=-1. Thus the design example gives -2 in both modes.
  4  char_eval char(q=3, n=2, s=(1) + (0)*i) with (-2 ; 1 * [1])
  5  char_eval_strict char(q=3, n=2, s=(1) + (0)*i) with (-2 ; 1 * [1])
  6  # Class coordinate is already sign-corrected: same value. Content 2 gives t=1 and value -1.
  7  char_eval char(q=3, n=2, s=(1) + (0)*i) with <2 ; [-1]>
  8  char_eval char(q=3, n=2, s=(1) + (0)*i) with (-2 ; 2 * [1])
  9  # Content 1/3 gives t=6; content 6/5 gives t=5/3. Choose inf=-6 and r=6/5 for exact t=5.
 10  char_eval char(q=3, n=2, s=(1) + (0)*i) with (-2 ; 1/3 * [1])
 11  char_eval char(q=3, n=2, s=(1) + (0)*i) with (-6 ; 6/5 * [1])
 12  # Diagonal -1 has u' = (-1)(-1)=1, t=1. Positive real part leaves the finite -1.
 13  char_eval char(q=3, n=2, s=(1) + (0)*i) with (-1 ; 1 * [-1])
 14  char_eval char(q=3, n=2, s=(1) + (0)*i) with (2 ; 1 * [-1])
 15  # Real chi_5(4) is even, so negative real part contributes +1; t=2.
 16  char_eval char(q=5, n=4, s=(1) + (0)*i) with (-2 ; 1 * [1])

fx-s-realroots · by tests/driver (imported by import_fixtures.py)SEALED

s-realroots: the real roots of an integer polynomial, through adf_roots_real

The driver fixtures: tests/driver/*.cmd with their expected lines, as claims

Script (driver lines)
the whole script, 81 lines
  1  # s-realroots: the real roots of an integer polynomial, through adf_roots_real
  2  # (include/adelefeld/roots.h, slice 3 of S.2; SPEC 9.1, the table "Real roots").  The polynomial
  3  # is written as its integer coefficients in decimal, separated by single spaces, the constant
  4  # term first.  The output is the balls in the order of the list, separated by "; ", each the
  5  # real-ball text of conventions 9.5 with the setting digits, and the word "none" for an empty
  6  # list.  A ball is printed as the driver prints a real part: the real coordinate of an adele
  7  # printed by adf_adele_get_str (include/adelefeld/text.h:160), the text between the "(" and the
  8  # " ; " (see tools/adf/README.md).
  9  #
 10  # The setting digits 1 is set in the first command of the script, and every expected line below
 11  # is derived from the accuracy contract of the balls and from conventions 9.5 alone, so that no
 12  # line depends on which isolating ball FLINT returns.  The two facts used are
 13  #   (1) the balls of the list have arb_rel_accuracy_bits >= max(prec, 2) = 64 (include/adelefeld/
 14  #       roots.h, decision S-D19), that is rad < |mid| 2^-64 < 1.5e-19 for a root of size about 1:
 15  #       arb_rel_error_bits is the difference between the position of the top bit of the radius and
 16  #       the top bit of the midpoint, plus one (refs/src/flint-3.0.1/arb.rst:500-504), so an
 17  #       accuracy of 64 bits gives a radius below |mid| 2^-64;
 18  #   (2) each ball holds one root of g, so its midpoint is within rad of that root.
 19  #!exit 1
 20  digits 1
 21  # X^2 - 2.  g* = X^2 - 2 has the two real roots -sqrt(2) and sqrt(2), and the list is in
 22  # increasing order.  For the positive root: |mid| is within 1.5e-19 of 1.4142135623..., so
 23  # X(mid) = 0.  Step 3 of conventions 9.5 with n = 1: q = X(mid) - 1 + 1 = 0, and
 24  # q = max(q, X(rad) - 1) = max(0, -21) = 0.  Step 4: M = round(mid, 0) = 1, and
 25  # E = rad + |M - mid| is in (0.41421, 0.41422), so R = ceil2(E) = 0.42.  Step 5 does not move q,
 26  # and the text is "1 +/- 0.42".  The ball of the negative root is its negative, so M = -1, E is in
 27  # the same interval and the text is "-1 +/- 0.42".  The two are printed in the order of the list.
 28  realroots -2 0 1
 29  # X^2 + 1 has no real root, so the list is empty and the answer is none.
 30  realroots 1 0 1
 31  # (X^2 - 2)^2 = X^4 - 4 X^2 + 4.  g* = X^2 - 2, the list holds the distinct real roots and a
 32  # multiplicity is not reported (SPEC 9.1), so the two balls are those of X^2 - 2 above.
 33  realroots 4 0 -4 0 1
 34  # X^4 + 1 is positive for every real x, so the list is empty: none.
 35  realroots 1 0 0 0 1
 36  # f = 0 is outside the domain of adf_roots_real (roots.h, "Statuses"), so the answer is
 37  # error: DOMAIN.
 38  realroots 0
 39  # A polynomial that is not a list of decimal integers separated by single spaces is not a
 40  # sentence of the operand form: error: PARSE.  So is an operand of two spaces between two
 41  # coefficients, and a second operand, of which the command takes none.
 42  realroots 1 2 x
 43  realroots 1  0 1
 44  realroots -2 0 1 with 7
 45  # The setting digits 20, the default of the driver (include/adelefeld/text.h:39,
 46  # ADF_DIGITS_DEFAULT).  The line below is the one line of this case that depends on which
 47  # isolating ball FLINT returns, and it is checked as follows.  The two balls that
 48  # adf_roots_real returns at prec 64 for X^2 - 2 are, exactly (lanes/drv-s/real_probe.c writes
 49  # them, arb_get_interval_fmpz_2exp of the ball and of its midpoint and radius):
 50  #   the ball of -sqrt(2): midpoint -240615969168004511545033772477625056927 2^-127, radius
 51  #                         268435457 2^-155, that is 154 bits of relative accuracy
 52  #   the ball of  sqrt(2): midpoint  240615969168004511545033772477625056927 2^-127, radius
 53  #                         1 2^-127, that is 126 bits
 54  # where the accuracy is the difference between the position of the top bit of the radius and of
 55  # the midpoint, plus one, as arb_rel_error_bits defines it
 56  # (refs/src/flint-3.0.1/arb.rst:500 to 504); both are above the 64 bits that S-D19 requires.
 57  # lanes/drv-s/real_check.py applies the algorithm of conventions 9.5 to those two pairs, in
 58  # exact rational arithmetic and written from the algorithm and not from the program, and gets the
 59  # text of the line: step 3 gives q = X(mid) - 19 = -20, M = round(mid, -20) is
 60  # 14142135623730950488 10^-19, the 20-digit rounding of the root, |M - mid| is 1.68872...e-21 and
 61  # rad is below 2^-126, so R = ceil2(rad + |M - mid|) = 1.7e-21, which fmt writes with an
 62  # exponent because X(R) = -21 < -4.  The script checks three more things: the printed interval
 63  # contains the exact ball, the exact end points of the ball give opposite signs of X^2 - 2 (the
 64  # test of solvers P3.8) and the ball is on one side of 0 with 2 strictly between the squares of
 65  # its end points.  So every property of the line that the specification fixes is derived, and
 66  # the ball is the only thing taken from FLINT.  As the two lines of tests/driver/09_settings.cmd,
 67  # this line depends on FLINT 3.0.1.
 68  digits 20
 69  realroots -2 0 1
 70  # The guard on printing a real ball reaches this command.  At the setting prec 100000 the accuracy
 71  # S-D19 asks for is 100000 bits, and the balls that FLINT returns for X^2 - 2 have then the radius
 72  # 1 2^-131071, whose binary exponent in the form of FLINT (x = m 2^e, 0.5 <= |m| < 1,
 73  # refs/src/flint-3.0.1/arf.rst:14 to 20) is -131070, above ADF_PRINT_EXP_MAX in absolute value.
 74  # A printer of the library that returns NULL is ADF_LIMIT for the driver (decision M1-D6,
 75  # include/adelefeld/text.h:32 to 38), and no byte of the line is written before the error, so the
 76  # answer is one line.  The radius is the one FLINT 3.0.1 gives: the accuracy contract alone asks
 77  # for more than 100000 bits, and a ball of exactly 100001 bits would have an exponent of -100000,
 78  # which the guard admits.  This line depends on FLINT 3.0.1, like the line above; the guard it
 79  # tests is the rule of the library and no bound of the driver.
 80  prec 100000
 81  realroots -2 0 1

  1  # s-recover: the rational of a residue class modulo m in a box, through adf_resid_reconstruct
  2  # (include/adelefeld/resid.h; SPEC 9.2, the second item, "From partial data").  The command is
  3  # "recover C mod M with A with B": the first operand is a finite ball of the value form, whose
  4  # canonical global triple is (C, M, 1), and it is the residue class P(M, C) of
  5  # adf_resid_set_fball_forget; A and B are the bounds of the numerator and of the denominator,
  6  # as exact rationals that must be integers.  The search limit of the driver is 1000 rounds.  A
  7  # solution is a pair of integers (n, d) with d > 0, gcd(n, d) = 1, gcd(d, M) = 1,
  8  # n = C d modulo M, |n| <= A and d <= B, and the answer is n/d in lowest terms.
  9  #
 10  # Every expected line of s-recover.out is derived here or in lanes/drv-s/expected_recover.py,
 11  # which enumerates the solutions of that definition over d in [1, B] and counts them.  The status
 12  # of each line is the status of Algorithm R of docs/proofs/solvers.md:256 to 324, whose steps are
 13  # quoted in the comment of the command they belong to.  Nothing is taken from the output of the
 14  # program.
 15  #!exit 1
 16  # P(19, 7) with A = 3, B = 3.  The enumeration gives exactly one solution, the pair (2, 3):
 17  # d = 1 needs n = 7 modulo 19 with |n| <= 3, which no integer meets; d = 2 needs n = 14 modulo 19,
 18  # that is n = -5, too large; d = 3 needs n = 21 = 2 modulo 19, and n = 2 is in the box, with
 19  # gcd(2, 3) = 1 and gcd(3, 19) = 1.  2 A B = 18 < 19, so at most one solution exists (P1.6 (c)) and
 20  # the answer is it: 2/3.
 21  recover 7 mod 19 with 3 with 3
 22  # P(19, 0) with A = 0, B = 3.  The only pair with |n| <= 0 is (0, 1), and 0 = 0 * 1 modulo 19,
 23  # gcd(0, 1) = 1 and gcd(1, 19) = 1, so 0/1 is a solution.  2 A B = 0 < 19, so it is the only
 24  # one, and the printer writes the exact zero as "0" (conventions 9.4, adf_rat_get_str).
 25  recover 0 mod 19 with 0 with 3
 26  # P(19, 18) = P(19, -1) with A = 1, B = 3.  d = 1 and n = -1 is a solution; 2 A B = 6 < 19 gives
 27  # at most one, so the answer is the pair (-1, 1).  The printer writes a rational whose denominator
 28  # is 1 as the numerator alone (conventions 9.4, adf_rat_get_str, "n or n/d in lowest terms"), so
 29  # the line is -1 and not -1/1.
 30  recover 18 mod 19 with 1 with 3
 31  # P(12, 6) with A = 1, B = 5.  The certificate pair of Lemma 1.4 is (R', T', R, T) = (6, 1, 0, -2),
 32  # and gcd(R, T) = 2 > 1, so the row 0/2 is not reduced and 2 A B = 10 < 12: step 6 of Algorithm R
 33  # gives NO_SOLUTION.  The enumeration agrees: the only candidates have d = 1 or 5 with
 34  # n = 6 d = 6 modulo 12, that is |n| >= 6 > 1.  This is the example of Remark 1 of P1.6.
 35  recover 6 mod 12 with 1 with 5
 36  # P(6, 5) with A = 1, B = 5.  The enumeration gives two solutions, -1/1 and 1/5.  The search of
 37  # step 7 of Algorithm R finds both: with (R', T', R, T) = (5, 1, 1, -1) and sigma = -1, the round
 38  # x = 1 gives y = 0 and the point (-1, 1), and the round x = 4 gives y = 1 (the range of y is
 39  # 0 <= y <= floor((4 * 1 + 1)/5) = 1) and the point (1, 5), so the status is NOT_UNIQUE.
 40  recover 5 mod 6 with 1 with 5
 41  # The same problem with B = 4: the point (1, 5) has d = 5 > 4 and is out of the box, so the
 42  # enumeration gives the single solution -1/1, X = floor(B / |T|) = 4 <= 1000, and step 8 of
 43  # Algorithm R returns OK with it.  The text is -1, as above.
 44  recover 5 mod 6 with 1 with 4
 45  # P(2, 1) with A = 2, B = 1.  A >= m, so step 3 of Algorithm R returns NOT_UNIQUE without a
 46  # search: c mod m / 1 = 1/1 and (c mod m - m) / 1 = -1/1 are two solutions (P1.6 (a)).
 47  recover 1 mod 2 with 2 with 1
 48  # P(2003, 1) with A = 3, B = 1001, the search limit 1000.  2003 is prime, 0 <= A = 3 < m, and
 49  # step 4 of Algorithm R stops at once: (R', T', R, T) = (2003, 0, 1, 1).  |T| = 1 <= B, and
 50  # 2 A B = 6006 >= m = 2003, so step 7 searches with X = floor(B / |T|) = 1001 > 1000 = ell.  In
 51  # the round x the range of y is max(0, ceil((x - 3)/2003)) = 0 to floor((x + 3)/2003) = 0 for
 52  # every x <= 1000, so the only point of the round is (x, x), which is reduced for x = 1 alone.
 53  # The search is cut with one point found, so step 8 returns NOT_DETERMINED: uniqueness is not
 54  # certified.  (The set of solutions is in fact the single pair (1, 1); the status says nothing
 55  # about existence, which is what SPEC 9.2 asks of it.)
 56  recover 1 mod 2003 with 3 with 1001

 60  # P(12, 6) with A = 1, B = 0.  The box is empty again, since B < 1: NO_SOLUTION.
 61  recover 6 mod 12 with 1 with 0

 63  # search.  A = 10^20 + 7, B = 10^20 + 9, n = -A, d = B, m = 2 A B + 1 = 2 A B + 1, and
 64  # c = n d^-1 modulo m, which is the number below.  gcd(n, d) = 1 (they differ by 16 and d is odd)
 65  # and gcd(d, m) = 1 (d is odd and m is odd), so (n, d) is a solution; 2 A B < m, so it is the only
 66  # one and step 6 of Algorithm R returns it.  The printer writes it in lowest terms with a positive

 71  # q = 1, the pair is (R', T', R, T) = (1000000, 1, 3, -1), so sigma = -1.  |T| = 1 <= B and
 72  # 2 A B = 6 * 10^30 >= m, so step 7 searches with X = floor(B / |T|) = 10^30 > 1000 = ell.  The
 73  # range of y in the round x is max(0, ceil((3 x - 3)/1000000)) = 0 to floor((3 x + 3)/1000000) = 0

 75  # x = 1 alone: the pair (-3, 1), and -3 = 1000000 modulo 1000003, so it is a solution.  One point
 76  # in a search that is cut: NOT_DETERMINED, as above.  The two bounds have 30 digits and are exact
 77  # integers of any size, since adf_resid_reconstruct takes fmpz arguments.
 78  recover 1000000 mod 1000003 with 3 with 1000000000000000000000000000000

s-roots: the roots of an integer polynomial at a prime, through adf_roots_padic

The driver fixtures: tests/driver/*.cmd with their expected lines, as claims

Script (driver lines)
the whole script, 53 lines
  1  # s-roots: the roots of an integer polynomial at a prime, through adf_roots_padic
  2  # (include/adelefeld/roots.h, slice 2 of S.2; SPEC 9.1, the table "Polynomial roots at a given
  3  # prime").  The polynomial is written as its integer coefficients in decimal, separated by
  4  # single spaces, the constant term first: "-2 0 1" is X^2 - 2.  The output is the roots in the
  5  # order of the list, separated by "; ", each as "A mod P^K" with A the centre in [0, P^K) and
  6  # K the precision of its certificate, and the word "none" for an empty complete list.
  7  #
  8  # Every expected line of s-roots.out is derived here or in lanes/drv-s/expected_roots.py, which
  9  # computes the centres by the digit rule a + t p^(k-1) with (a + t p^(k-1))^2 = c mod p^k and
 10  # no other.  Nothing is taken from the output of the program.
 11  #!exit 1
 12  # X^2 - 2 in Z_7 at prec_p = 5.  The squares modulo 7 are 0, 1, 2 and 4, and 2 = 3^2 = 4^2 mod 7,
 13  # so there are two roots modulo 7 and both are simple, so both lift to Z_7.  7^5 = 16807.  The
 14  # digit rule from a = 3: (3 + 7 t)^2 = 2 mod 49 gives 7 + 42 t = 0 mod 49, that is t = 1, so 10;
 15  # from 10 to mod 343: (10 + 49 t)^2 = 2 mod 343, and so on, which gives the two centres 4567 and
 16  # 12240.  The check: 4567^2 - 2 = 16807 * 1241 and 12240^2 - 2 = 16807 * 8914, both centres are
 17  # 3 and 4 mod 7, and 4567 + 12240 = 16807.  For each, s = v_7(2 alpha) = 0, so K = max(5, 1) = 5.
 18  roots -2 0 1 with 7 with 5
 19  # X^2 - 2 in Z_5 at prec_p = 5.  The squares modulo 5 are 0, 1 and 4; 2 is not one of them, so
 20  # no root of the polynomial is in Z_5, the list is complete and empty, and the answer is none.
 21  roots -2 0 1 with 5 with 5
 22  # 27 X at 3.  g* is X, since 27 is removed and the leading coefficient is made positive.  The
 23  # only root is 0 with s = v_3(g'(0)) = v_3(1) = 0, so K = max(5, 1) = 5 and the centre is 0.
 24  roots 0 27 with 3 with 5
 25  # (X - 1)(X + 2) = X^2 + X - 2 in Z_7 at prec_p = 5.  The roots are 1 and -2 = 5 mod 7.  For
 26  # both s = v_7(2 alpha + 1) = v_7(3) = v_7(-3) = 0, so K = 5.  The centre of the root 1 is 1; the
 27  # centre of the root -2 is the lift of 5 mod 7 to 7^5, and -2 = 16807 - 2 = 16805 is already in
 28  # [0, 16807) and is the root, so the centre is 16805.
 29  roots -2 1 1 with 7 with 5
 30  # X^2 - 1 in Z_2 at prec_p = 5, so 2^5 = 32.  The two roots are 1 and -1, and they are both
 31  # congruent to 1 modulo 2, so the one root of the polynomial modulo 2 gives two balls in Z_2.
 32  # For both s = v_2(g'(alpha)) = v_2(2 alpha) = 1, so K = max(5, 2) = 5.  The centres are 1 and
 33  # -1 = 31 in [0, 32), in increasing order; the check is 31^2 - 1 = 960 = 30 * 32.
 34  roots -1 0 1 with 2 with 5
 35  # (X - 1)(X + 2)^2 = X^3 + 3 X^2 - 4 in Z_7 at prec_p = 5.  f is not squarefree; the list is the
 36  # list of the roots of g* = (X - 1)(X + 2) (decision S-D13, include/adelefeld/roots.h), and a
 37  # multiplicity is never reported (SPEC 9.1).  So the answer is the same two centres as above.
 38  roots -4 0 3 1 with 7 with 5
 39  # A prime of 64 bits: 18446744073709551557 = 2^64 - 59, which adf_place_prime certifies with
 40  # n_is_prime (include/adelefeld/place.h:34).  (X - 3)(X - 5)(X - 7) = X^3 - 15 X^2 + 71 X - 105
 41  # is squarefree and has the planted integer roots 3, 5 and 7.  g' = 3 X^2 - 30 X + 71 has the
 42  # values -8, -4 and 20 at them, none of which the prime divides, so s = 0 for all three, K is
 43  # max(3, 1) = 3 and the centre is the root itself, the prime being larger than 7.  Above
 44  # ADF_ROOTS_P_EVAL_MAX the roots modulo p are found by the degree of gcd(h, X^p - X), which is
 45  # what this case exercises; p = 7 and p = 3 of the other cases are below it and use the
 46  # evaluation at every residue.
 47  roots -105 71 -15 1 with 18446744073709551557 with 3
 48  # A constant polynomial: g* is 1 (roots.h, the normalised polynomial), which has no root in any
 49  # place, so the complete list is empty and the answer is none.
 50  roots 5 with 7 with 5
 51  # f = 0 is outside the domain of adf_roots_padic (roots.h, "Statuses", edit E-C1), and the driver
 52  # decides it when it reads the polynomial: error: DOMAIN.
 53  roots 0 with 7 with 5
